30:["$","p",null,{"children":"Both also leave the codeword subspace invariant, so a state prepared on a codeword stays on codewords. The choice matters only for states that leave that subspace, for example through noise. There the projected operator suppresses the evolution, because its projectors fail, whereas the unprojected one keeps acting. Neither behavior is a physical requirement. The unprojected operator is only defined on the one-hot subspace, so choosing it is a modeling decision and not a free optimization.","className":"group paragraph-el text-text-primary mx-0"}]

47:["$","p",null,{"children":["Two details are worth knowing. First, the qubit operator must have real coefficients, because ",["$","$L2",null,{"href":"/docs/api/qiskit/qiskit.circuit.library.PauliEvolutionGate","children":["$","code",null,{"children":"PauliEvolutionGate","className":"text-code-02 inline px-4 bg-background border border-transparent dark:border-border-subtle-00 [overflow-wrap:anywhere] group-[.pre-el]:text-code-02 group-[.pre-el]:inline-block group-[.pre-el]:whitespace-pre group-[.pre-el]:p-12 group-[.pre-el]:pr-[50px] group-[.pre-el]:bg-inherit dark:group-[.pre-el]:border-transparent group-[.paragraph-el]:whitespace-normal"}]}]," rejects an imaginary part.
